## 2. Keyless loadability checks in other repos

Four repos install a real CLI in CI without a credential. Only one of them
exercises Codex plugin loading.

- **trailofbits/skills** is the only repo that proves both runtimes load
  the plugins. `.github/workflows/validate.yml` installs Claude Code at
  `@latest` and Codex pinned at `0.146.0`, then runs two scripts.
  - `.github/scripts/check_claude_loadability.py` sets `HOME` and
    `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` to a temp directory. It runs
    `claude plugin validate --strict` on the marketplace and on each
    `plugins/<name>/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, then
    `claude plugin marketplace add <repo>`,
    `claude plugin list --available --json`,
    `claude plugin install <name>@<marketplace>`, and
    `claude plugin list --json`. It fails on missing plugins, any entry in
    a plugin's `errors`, or an MCP server set that differs from the
    manifest.
  - `.github/scripts/check_codex_loadability.py` sets `HOME` and
    `CODEX_HOME` to a temp directory and writes
    `[features] plugins = true, plugin_hooks = true` to `config.toml`. It
    starts `codex app-server --listen stdio:// --enable plugins --enable
    plugin_hooks` with
    `CODEX_APP_SERVER_DISABLE_MANAGED_CONFIG=1`. It sends JSON-RPC
    `initialize`, `initialized`, `plugin/list` with
    `{"cwds": [repo], "marketplaceKinds": ["local"]}`, and `plugin/read`
    per plugin. It fails on `marketplaceLoadErrors`, a missing plugin, or
    a skill count that differs from the `SKILL.md` files on disk.
  - The workflow comment on the Claude install explains the choice: "a
    Claude Code release that breaks plugin loading turns every open PR red
    at once, with no commit here to explain it. That is the intended trade.
    A pin nobody remembers to bump is worse."
  - `.github/scripts/validate_plugin_metadata.py` forbids `.agents/` and
    `.codex-plugin/`: "Claude marketplace metadata is the single canonical
    source; Codex and other runtimes read it through that compatibility."
- **addyosmani/agent-skills** `.github/workflows/test-plugin-install.yml`
  runs `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code` unpinned, then
  `claude plugin validate .`, `claude plugin marketplace add ./`, and
  `claude plugin install agent-skills@addy-agent-skills --scope user`. It
  does not check Codex.
- **EveryInc/compound-engineering-plugin** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins
  `CLAUDE_CODE_VERSION: 2.1.220`, caches the install keyed on that version,
  and runs `bun run plugin:validate`, which is
  `claude plugin validate --strict .claude-plugin/marketplace.json && claude
  plugin validate --strict .claude-plugin/plugin.json`. `AGENTS.md` warns:
  "Do not use `claude plugin validate .`: that resolves this repo as a
  marketplace only ... and skips plugin-root checks."
- **JuliusBrussee/caveman** `.github/workflows/agent-conformance.yml`
  installs Claude and Codex, but `agents/probe-installed.mjs` checks only
  version and help output. It does not load plugins.
- **wshobson/agents** `validate.yml` runs real OpenCode, Antigravity, and
  Pi CLIs against generated artifacts. It does not install Claude or
  Codex.

The model-running workflows (gstack evals, trailofbits claude-review,
octopus, and the `claude-code-action` users) all need a key or OAuth token.
trailofbits reduces the key to a boolean in one step so the job "go[es]
quiet rather than red" when the key is missing.

## Local checks against adhd-unslop

All checks ran against `git clone --local` of this repo at `fc308bd`, in
`$S/au-copy`, so no CLI could write into the working tree. Every command
unset `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, or `OPENAI_API_KEY`
and `CODEX_API_KEY`, and used a fresh config directory under `$S`. No
`auth.json` was copied. `git status --porcelain` on the real repo showed
only `design/idea1.md` afterwards, before this file was written.

### a. `claude plugin validate`

Command form:

```sh
env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY -u CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN HOME=$T \
  CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=$T/claude claude plugin validate [--strict] <path> </dev/null
```

| Path | Resolved as | Default | `--strict` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| repo root | `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` | pass, exit 0 | pass, exit 0 |
| `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` | marketplace | pass | pass |
| `plugins/adhd-unslop` | its `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | pass | pass |
| `plugins/adhd-unslop/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | plugin | pass | pass |
| `plugins/au-i-have-adhd/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | plugin | pass | pass |
| `plugins/au-unslop/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` | plugin | pass | pass |

Each run printed `Validating ... manifest: <path>` and `✔ Validation
passed`. Validating the repo root checks only the marketplace, which
confirms the EveryInc warning. `validate` also has `--json`.

Negative cases on a throwaway copy show what validate catches:

| Change | Default | `--strict` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `hooks.json` is invalid JSON | error, exit 1: "At runtime this breaks the entire plugin load." | exit 1 |
| `SKILL.md` frontmatter has no `description` | warning, exit 0 | exit 1 |
| Marketplace entry version `9.9.9`, `plugin.json` says `0.2.2` | warning, exit 0: "plugin.json wins ... the entry version is silently ignored" | exit 1 |
| Unknown key in `plugin.json` | warning, exit 0 | exit 1 |
| `CLAUDE.md` at a plugin root | warning, exit 0: "not loaded as project context" | exit 1 |
| Unknown hook event in `hooks.json` | warning, exit 0: "entry ignored at runtime" | exit 1 |
| Marketplace `source` points at a missing directory | pass, exit 0 | pass, exit 0 |
| Skill `name` is `Not_Kebab Case` | not tested in default | pass, exit 0 |

Only `--strict` turns most problems into failures. Validate does not check
that marketplace sources exist, so an install step is still needed.

trailofbits' script, copied unchanged to `$S/checks/`, also passes:

```sh
env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY -u CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN TMPDIR=$S/checks \
  python3 check_claude_loadability.py $S/au-copy --claude-bin ~/.local/bin/claude
# loaded Claude marketplace adhd-unslop with 3 plugins and 0 MCP servers
# All Claude loadability checks passed
```

Installing only the composed plugin pulls in both dependencies without a
key:

```sh
claude plugin marketplace add $S/au-copy
# ✔ Successfully added marketplace: adhd-unslop (declared in user settings)
claude plugin install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop
# ✔ Successfully installed plugin: adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop (scope: user)
#   (+ 2 dependencies: au-i-have-adhd, au-unslop)
claude plugin list --json
# three entries, versions 0.2.2, 0.1.0, 0.1.1, each with
# "installPath": "<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/plugins/cache/adhd-unslop/<plugin>/<version>"
claude plugin details adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop
# Skills (1)  adhd-unslop
# Hooks (1)  SessionStart  (harness-only, no model context cost)
# Always-on:   ~79 tok
```

The cache directory for `adhd-unslop/0.2.2` held real copies of all 16
shipped files. This local-path install copied files into the cache. D21 and
the platform facts table say a local path loads plugins in place. See
"Claims to test". `plugin details` has no `--json` option.

GitHub mode also works without a key:
`claude plugin marketplace add rubenvarela/adhd-unslop#main` cloned over
HTTPS, and `claude plugin install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop` installed three
plugins into the cache.

codex
The new runtime tests confirm `clear` and normal compaction behavior. I found one documented Codex exit path that still drops the bundle, plus an upgrade-test gap.

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba design/STRUCTURE-v2.md | sed -n '50,180p;270,360p;370,425p'" in /Users/ruben.varela/stow/documents-ruben/Development/00-Scratch/2026-09-12--adhd-unslop
 succeeded in 0ms:
    50	
    51	- No repo out of 25 uses plugin `dependencies`. Repos that share text
    52	  between plugins copy it at build time and check the copies for drift
    53	  (`03-multi-plugin-marketplaces.md`).
    54	- Dependencies failed in practice. `claude plugin update` did not install
    55	  newly declared dependencies, and a repeated install added one missing
    56	  dependency per run (D16). Codex has no dependencies at all, so Codex users
    57	  ran three install commands and relied on a warning hook.
    58	- The hook already carries its own copy of both texts (D12), so a skill that
    59	  also reads its own copy adds no new kind of duplication.
    60	
    61	What the user asked for still holds. The text comes only from our own pinned
    62	copies, never a third-party marketplace. i-have-adhd stays available as its
    63	own plugin. A new plugin reuses i-have-adhd by listing it, and the build
    64	copies the text in.
    65	
    66	Evidence that the mechanism works:
    67	
    68	- A skill that reads `references/extra.md` next to its `SKILL.md` read the
    69	  right file, not a decoy, in Codex 0.154.0 and 0.157.1 (`07` C2).
    70	- In Claude Code, `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` resolves to the versioned cache
    71	  path. A bare relative path also works, because every skill body starts
    72	  with its base directory (`08` L2).
    73	- Codex does not cut our skill. A 12,000-byte skill arrived whole on both
    74	  versions. Codex cuts at 8,000 bytes only for a plugin with a root
    75	  `plugin.json` that uses the agent-plugins `$schema` (`07` C1).
    76	
    77	Result: `claude plugin install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop` or
    78	`codex plugin add adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop` installs everything. Updates touch
    79	one plugin.
    80	
    81	#### Reference contract
    82	
    83	- Path: `plugins/<plugin>/skills/<skill>/references/<upstream>.md`, one file
    84	  per upstream the skill lists.
    85	- Content: the same block the hook uses, byte for byte. That is the line
    86	  `<!-- BEGIN upstream <name> <repo>/<path> @<commit> -->`, then the
    87	  upstream `SKILL.md` with its frontmatter removed and a final newline
    88	  ensured, then `<!-- END upstream <name> -->`.
    89	- Tests compare each reference file with that block built from
    90	  `upstream/<name>/SKILL.md`, and require it to match the hook chunk that
    91	  carries the same upstream.
    92	- The skill tells Claude Code to read
    93	  `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/references/<upstream>.md` and Codex to read
    94	  `references/<upstream>.md` relative to its `SKILL.md`. It reads nothing
    95	  else and never searches the disk.
    96	- The skill skips a reference whose BEGIN line is already in the
    97	  conversation, which happens when the always-on hook delivered it.
    98	
    99	#### Versioning rule
   100	
   101	A plugin ships an upstream's text when it is that upstream's vendored
   102	mirror, when one of its skills lists the upstream in `references`, or when
   103	its always-on chunks include it. `sync.mjs` raises the patch version of
   104	every such plugin once per bump run. A future `presentation` plugin that
   105	lists i-have-adhd therefore gets a new version whenever i-have-adhd
   106	changes. The CI version gate catches every other change to shipped files.
   107	
   108	### P2. Vendored plugins are byte-for-byte mirrors
   109	
   110	`au-i-have-adhd` and `au-unslop` ship the upstream `SKILL.md` unchanged,
   111	frontmatter included. Neither upstream skill folder has an
   112	`agents/openai.yaml`, so the build generates one that matches the Claude Code
   113	setting: `allow_implicit_invocation: false` when the upstream frontmatter
   114	sets `disable-model-invocation: true`, and `true` otherwise.
   115	
   116	- This reverses D7, whose only reason was that `adhd-unslop` loaded these
   117	  skills through the Skill tool. Nothing loads them now.
   118	- A mirror that changes nothing is simpler to trust and to test. The test
   119	  becomes one comparison of the whole file.
   120	- Typed invocation still works. i-have-adhd stays user-only, as upstream
   121	  ships it. unslop stays model-invocable, as upstream ships it.
   122	- The `au-` prefix stays (D6). The name clash it prevents still exists.
   123	- The plugins are optional. `adhd-unslop` never needs them.
   124	
   125	### P3. The always-on hook keeps embedded chunks
   126	
   127	The hook keeps three chunks printed from files inside `adhd-unslop`.
   128	
   129	- Always-on SessionStart injection is the proven pattern: superpowers,
   130	  ponytail, caveman, ECC, and planning-with-files use it.
   131	- Codex has no other mechanism a plugin can set. `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` and
   132	  `developer_instructions` belong to the user (`06-official-docs.md`).
   133	- Three chunks are needed because Claude Code saves a value over 10,000
   134	  characters to a file and shows the model a path and the first 2,000
   135	  characters (`08` L5). The cap is documented at
   136	  code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#json-output and cannot be raised.
   137	
   138	Changes:
   139	
   140	- The matcher becomes `startup|clear|compact`.
   141	  - A resumed session keeps its earlier injection in both runtimes, so a
   142	    `resume` match added a second copy of about 24,000 characters (`07` C4,
   143	    `08` L1).
   144	  - A forked Claude Code session sends `source: "fork"` and carries the
   145	    parent's copy (`08` L1), so `fork` needs no match.
   146	  - `clear` and `compact` each leave exactly one complete copy, all three
   147	    END lines present, in both runtimes (`09`). In Claude Code that holds
   148	    for `/compact`, `/clear`, and auto-compaction. In Codex it holds for
   149	    `/clear`, manual compaction, and auto-compaction before or during a
   150	    turn. `codex exec` has no slash commands, so it reaches `compact` only
   151	    through auto-compaction and never reaches `clear`.
   152	  - One Codex gap: Codex fires `compact` only when the next turn starts,
   153	    and keeps the pending source in memory. A user who compacts and quits
   154	    before sending a message resumes later with no copy. The old matcher
   155	    gave one copy there. Invoking `$adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop` restores the
   156	    rules, and the README says so. Every other resume is one copy instead
   157	    of two, so the trade favors dropping `resume`.
   158	  - Claude Code orders the chunks by which handler finishes first, while
   159	    Codex keeps the declared order (`09`). The header already accepts any
   160	    order.
   161	- Handlers keep `additionalContextLimit: 5000`. The built chunks were not
   162	  tested against the Codex default with non-ASCII text, and unslop contains
   163	  `→`. Keeping the key costs nothing and avoids that risk.
   164	- Header wording becomes statements of fact. The Claude Code docs warn that
   165	  imperative hook text can trigger prompt-injection defenses. The header
   166	  adds that a chunk counts only when its END line is present.
   167	- The Codex manifest names the hooks file explicitly, as every repo that
   168	  ships a Codex plugin hook does. Trust survives the explicit path (`07` C3).
   169	
   170	`ADHD_UNSLOP_ALWAYS` semantics:
   171	
   172	- Unset or empty: the flag files decide, as today.
   173	- `0`, `false`, or `off`, in any case: injection is off for this process,
   174	  even when a flag file exists.
   175	- `1`, `true`, or `on`: injection is on without a flag file.
   176	- Any other value is treated as unset. The doctor reports it.
   177	
   178	Codex keys hook trust by plugin, hooks file path, event, group, and handler
   179	index, and checks a hash of the handler (`07` C3). The matcher change and
   180	the removed dependency group change the hook definitions once. They ship in
   270	skill's frontmatter sets `disable-model-invocation: true`, and its
   271	`openai.yaml` sets `allow_implicit_invocation: false`.
   272	
   273	Checks:
   274	
   275	- the runtime and the plugin root the script runs from
   276	- the installed `adhd-unslop` version, from the plugin root's manifest
   277	- the `au-` plugins, listed as optional independent installs, never WARN
   278	  or FAIL when absent
   279	- which always-on flag files exist, and the value of `ADHD_UNSLOP_ALWAYS`,
   280	  with a WARN for an unrecognized value
   281	- the chunk hashes and sizes, reusing the launcher's validation
   282	- Codex: whether `[features]` in `config.toml` turns hooks off. Codex turns
   283	  hooks on by default (`07` C7).
   284	- a `~/.agents/skills` entry named `adhd-unslop`, or a link into a copy of
   285	  this repo, which creates a duplicate skill name (D20)
   286	
   287	It reports the installed hook definitions and tells Codex users to confirm
   288	trust in `/hooks`. It does not parse Codex's trust records, whose layout is
   289	not documented.
   290	
   291	Precedent: ECC, gstack, claude-octopus, planning-with-files, oh-my-codex, and
   292	oh-my-claudecode ship doctors. Silent hooks make a broken install look like
   293	always-on being off.
   294	
   295	### P9. CI
   296	
   297	`verify.yml`, renamed from `daily-build.yml`, runs on pull requests, pushes
   298	to `main`, and manual dispatch.
   299	
   300	- `checks`: `sync.mjs --check`, `build.mjs --check` with the stray-file
   301	  rule, unit tests, and on pull requests a version gate. The gate fails when
   302	  files under `plugins/<name>/` differ from the merge base and that plugin's
   303	  version did not rise. The pattern comes from expo.
   304	- `load`: installs pinned Claude Code and Codex versions and runs
   305	  `tools/load-check.mjs` with no keys, in throwaway homes.
   306	  - Claude Code: `validate --strict` on the marketplace and each plugin,
   307	    `marketplace add` of the checkout, `install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`,
   308	    and `list --json`. It asserts that only `adhd-unslop` installs, with the
   309	    expected version and no errors, and that its cache copy holds the skill,
   310	    both reference files, and the hooks.
   311	  - Codex: `codex plugin marketplace add` of the checkout and `codex plugin
   312	    add adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`. It asserts the cache copy the same way.
   313	    Then `app-server` with `initialize`, `plugin/list`, and `plugin/read`,
   314	    asserting full skill names, hook keys, and versions for all three
   315	    plugins. The pattern comes from trailofbits.
   316	- `workflows`: actionlint and zizmor, from trailofbits and gstack.
   317	
   318	`cli-drift.yml` runs the load check daily against the latest CLI releases
   319	and opens or updates an issue on failure. The pattern comes from caveman.
   320	
   321	`upstream-bump.yml` keeps D18's design. It dispatches `verify.yml` by its
   322	new name and adds `sync.mjs --verify-remote`, which refetches the pinned
   323	files and compares hashes.
   324	
   325	Dependabot updates GitHub Actions weekly with a cooldown. Workflows pin
   326	actions by commit SHA with a version comment. `.gitattributes` sets LF line
   327	endings and marks generated files.
   328	
   329	### P10. End-to-end tests and migration
   330	
   331	`tests/e2e/run.sh` stays local, because model-driven checks need signed-in
   332	CLIs. It gains:
   333	
   334	- Claude Code: `/adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop` with always-on off reads both
   335	  reference files, seen as Read tool calls on the `references/` paths.
   336	- Codex: the same, seen as reads of the cache `references/` paths in the
   337	  session log.
   338	- Typed `/au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd` and `$au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd`
   339	  still work with the restored upstream frontmatter.
   340	- Always-on delivers one complete bundle at startup, with each END line
   341	  present, and a resumed session holds one copy, not two.
   342	- An upgrade from 0.2.2, served from a local git server. A small `git
   343	  http-backend` on localhost worked as a git marketplace with real cache
   344	  paths (`08`). The script installs 0.2.2 with the flag on, moves the
   345	  server's `main` to the new commit, runs the documented update commands,
   346	  and checks that always-on delivers one bundle afterwards and that the
   347	  hooks definitions changed once.
   348	- `claude plugin prune` removes `au-` plugins that were installed as
   349	  dependencies of 0.2.2 and keeps an `au-` plugin the user installed by
   350	  name.
   351	
   352	Documented migration from 0.2.2:
   353	
   354	- Claude Code: `claude plugin marketplace update adhd-unslop`, then
   355	  `claude plugin update adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`, then optionally `claude
   356	  plugin prune` to remove the `au-` plugins that 0.2.2 installed as
   357	  dependencies.
   358	- Codex: `codex plugin marketplace upgrade adhd-unslop`, then `codex plugin
   359	  add adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`, then review the changed hooks once in
   360	  `/hooks`. Remove the `au-` plugins with `codex plugin remove` only if they
   370	
   371	`README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `NOTICE.md`, and `DECISIONS.md` change to match.
   372	In `DECISIONS.md`, D4, D7, D9, D12, D15, D16, D17, and D21 are rewritten, the
   373	platform facts table takes the new test results, and new entries record P3
   374	to P9. `design/idea1.md` records its outcome. The README drops the
   375	`~/.codex/AGENTS.md` fallback, because hooks are the supported always-on
   376	path and the fallback named a skill Codex hides from the model.

    27	
    28	Conclusion: with `startup|clear|compact`, every clear, and every
    29	compaction followed by another turn in the same process, ends with exactly
    30	one full copy of the injected text in both runtimes. The change removes the
    31	resume duplicate. It has one cost, in Codex only. If a user compacts and
    32	quits before sending another message, a later `resume` has no copy, where
    33	the old matcher gave one (X7). The model then has only what the compaction
    34	summary kept.
    35	
    36	## Method
    37	
    38	- `$TS` below is the session scratchpad folder `tests-sources/`. The
    39	  scratchpad is session storage and may be deleted later.
    40	- `gen.mjs` builds a local git marketplace `zsmkt` in the Claude-only layout.
    41	  Research 07 C8 showed that Codex installs it too. It has two plugins.
    42	  - `zs-one`: group 1, matcher `startup|clear|compact`, one handler that
    43	    prints `Fixture context line: ZSONE-<source>-n<count> is the session
    44	    fixture token.` Group 2 has no matcher and only logs, so `resume`,
    45	    `fork`, or anything unexpected still shows up in the log.
    46	  - `zs-three`: one group, matcher `startup|clear|compact`, three handlers,
    47	    like the real `hooks.json`. Handler k prints about 7,140 characters:
    48	    `Fixture chunk k of 3 begins: ZSCkSTART-<tag>`, filler with
    49	    `ZSCkMID-<tag>` in the middle, and a last line `Fixture chunk k of 3 END:
    50	    ZSCkEND-<tag>`. The tag is `-<source>-n<count>`.
    51	- Every handler uses the real launcher pattern, `node -e` with
    52	  `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT||PLUGIN_ROOT`, and prints plain text as
    53	  `always-on.mjs` does. Each handler keeps its own counter and appends its
    54	  stdin JSON to `<config home>/zs-log/hooklog.jsonl`.
    55	- A "full copy" means START, MID, and END of one tag inside one unbroken
   340	
   341	```
   342	exec /bin/zsh -lc 'seq 1 4000' ... succeeded
   343	context compacted
   344	hook: SessionStart (x5) ... hook: SessionStart Completed (x5)
   345	exec /bin/zsh -lc 'echo second' ... succeeded
   346	DONE
   347	```
   348	
   349	Rollout, 0.157.1. The 0.154.0 rollout had the same shape.
   350	
   351	```
   352	L20  token_count last_input=18266
   353	L22  compacted replacement_history=6 items: 3 developer (initial context, no ZS text),
   354	     2 user, 1 compaction
   355	L28-31 developer: ZSONE + ZSC1 ZSC2 ZSC3 compact-n2
   356	L36  token_count last_input=18452
   357	=> one full copy, compact-n2
   358	```
   359	
   360	The `compact` hook ran in the middle of the turn, right after the
   361	compaction and before the next model request. That matches `turn.rs:633`.
   362	
   363	### X5 and X6. Pre-turn auto-compaction on `exec resume`
   364	
   365	The first run filled the history and set no limit. Then `codex exec -c
   366	model_auto_compact_token_limit=... resume --last "<question>"`.
   367	
   368	- 0.154.0, X5: after `seq 1 4000` the input was 29,751, with a limit of
   369	  24,000. The resume turn logged `resume` (catch-all only), compacted, then
   370	  ran `compact` x5 in the same turn. Visible after compaction: one full copy,
   371	  compact-n2. Input 19,065. The reply listed the startup tokens too, from
   372	  the compaction summary.
   373	- 0.157.1, X5: no compaction. The model ran `seq` inside a code-mode script
   374	  and kept only a summary, so the history stayed at 18,411 tokens. The
   375	  resume turn fired only the catch-all `resume` handler and showed the one
   376	  startup copy.
   377	- 0.157.1, X6: the first turn asked for the integers 1 to 2500 with no
   378	  command. The resume ran with a limit of 21,000. It compacted before
   379	  sampling, logged `resume` then `compact`, and ended with one full copy,
   380	  compact-n2. Input 18,407.
   381	
   382	### X7. Compact, quit, then resume
   383	
   384	Codex keeps queued SessionStart sources only in memory
   385	(`pending_session_start_sources` in `core/src/state/session.rs`). The only
   386	callers are the session start, which queues `resume` on a resume
   387	(`session.rs:1899` [1639]), and `replace_compacted_history`. Nothing
   388	rebuilds a pending `compact` from the rollout. Two cases, each in a fresh
   389	home:
   390	
   391	- Case A: `appsrv.py ... start "turn:say ok" compact`, and the driver
   392	  exits. Then `codex exec ... resume <thread-id> "<question>"` from the same
   393	  cwd.
   394	- Case B: the same, with one more turn, `say ok again`, after the compact
   395	  and before the exit.
   396	
   397	```
   398	0.157.1 A  hook log: startup x5, resume (catch-all only)
   399	           visible after compaction: exact ZSONE [], full chunks [], input 13,878
   400	           reply: "ZS", TOTAL: 1
   401	0.154.0 A  hook log: startup x5, resume (catch-all only)
   402	           visible after compaction: exact ZSONE [], full chunks [], input 14,705
   403	           reply: the 10 startup tokens, recalled from the compaction summary
   404	0.157.1 B  hook log: startup x5, compact x5, resume (catch-all only)
   405	           visible: one full copy, compact-n2, input 19,719
   406	0.154.0 B  hook log: startup x5, compact x5, resume (catch-all only)
   407	           visible: one full copy, compact-n2, input 20,516
   408	```
   409	
   410	In case A the resumed input is 3,900 to 4,400 tokens below the first turn,
   411	which held a copy.
   412	With the new matcher, a thread compacted as its last action comes back
   413	with no rules, only the summary. The old matcher would have injected one
   414	copy on that resume, and two in case B. Claude Code has no such gap,
   415	because it runs the `compact` hook during `/compact` and saves the output
   416	in the transcript. K2 showed that copy reloading on resume.
   417

   419	
   420	1. Claude Code orders parallel handler output by completion time. The

     5	## Blocking
     6	
     7	1. P1: “the build copies the text in” has no reference contract. C2 proves a relative file can be read, not that copies stay byte-accurate, omit frontmatter consistently, or trigger version bumps. Define generated reference contents and paths, test them against pinned bodies, and make `sync.mjs` bump every plugin with `references`, including future `presentation`.
     8	
     9	2. P3: “The matcher becomes `startup|clear|compact`” relies on untested `clear` and `compact` behavior. Both result files explicitly leave them untested. Add real-CLI tests in both runtimes that prove one complete bundle arrives on each source before freezing this matcher.
    10	
    11	3. P3: “any value under Claude Code's 10,000-character cap fits” overstates C6. Non-ASCII output was not tested, and shipped unslop text contains `→`. Test the built chunks with the default Codex limit and end markers, including non-ASCII, before removing `additionalContextLimit`.
    12	
    13	4. P6: “The build deletes [stray files].” A normal build must not delete arbitrary untracked files under `plugins/`. Make `--check` fail on orphans, make normal build write expected files only, and require an explicit `--prune` for deletion.
    14	
    15	5. P7: “The Codex marketplace drops `policy.products`.” This expands distribution from Codex to ChatGPT and Atlas. The stated scope is Claude Code and Codex. Keep `products: ["CODEX"]` unless the user explicitly approves those additional products.
    16	
    17	6. P8: “installed versions of the three plugins” contradicts P1 and P10, which make `au-*` optional. The doctor must require only `adhd-unslop`; report `au-*` as independent optional installs, not WARN or FAIL when absent.
    18	
    19	7. P8: “whether `config.toml` has trust entries” is not proven. C3 established trust-key behavior, but the results explicitly did not test trust recorded through `/hooks`. Do not make private `config.toml` layout a doctor verdict. Report installed hook definitions and direct the user to `/hooks` until that path is tested.
    20	
    21	8. P9: Codex `initialize`, `plugin/list`, and `plugin/read` validate marketplace discovery, not the user install path. Add a clean-home `marketplace add` plus `plugin add` check, then assert that 0.3.0 installs only `adhd-unslop` and exposes its skill and hooks.
    22	
    23	9. P9: The proposed tests do not cover P1, P2, or the hook migration. Extend E2E to prove both referenced upstream bodies are read, restored vendored frontmatter preserves typed invocation, and always-on arrives once after a 0.2.2 upgrade.
    24	
    25	10. P10: The migration is not actionable. “can remove” is not a tested path. Document and test exact Claude and Codex update commands from 0.2.2 with flags on and old Codex trust present, then verify: new hook definitions require one re-trust, always-on resumes afterward, and pruning or removing `au-*` does not remove standalone installs.
    26	
    27	## Do not build
    28	
    29	1. P4 forced output styles. They override the user's selected style and create a Claude-only delivery path.
    30	
    31	2. P5 per-session mode tracking. A hook on every prompt and another Codex trust entry do not justify fixing a recoverable edge case.
    32	
    33	3. P8 checks for an upstream plugin's undocumented always-on flag or private trust-state schema. They will create false diagnostics.
    34	
    35	4. Subagent injection. It triples the bundle cost per subagent without a stated need.
    36	
    37	5. A separate Codex hooks file. L4 supports the shared file, and a second file adds drift and another trust migration.
    38	
    39	## Missing
    40	
    41	1. A formal generated-source schema for authored skills, references, hook sources, READMEs, licenses, and notices.
    42	
    43	2. Updates to `README.md`, D4, D7, D9, D15 to D17, D21, and all tests that currently require dependencies and rewritten frontmatter.
    44	
    45	3. A versioning rule for a future `presentation` plugin when i-have-adhd changes.
    46	
    47	4. Defined `ADHD_UNSLOP_ALWAYS` semantics: accepted values, precedence over flag files, and behavior for invalid values.
    48	
    49	## Optional
    50	
    51	1. P1 and P2 are directionally justified. Claude dependency updates failed in real use, Codex has no dependencies, and byte-for-byte mirrors are simpler once `adhd-unslop` no longer loads them.
    52	
    53	2. Keep both native manifests, explicit Codex hooks path, marketplace-entry version removal, orphan detection, pinned CLI checks, and a latest-CLI drift job after the install-path coverage is added.

## Round 6 items

1. Resolved. P1 defines generated reference paths, bytes, tests, and bump propagation.
2. Resolved. Research 09 proves `clear` and normal `compact` delivery in both runtimes.
3. Resolved. P3 retains `additionalContextLimit: 5000`.
4. Resolved. Only `--prune` deletes orphans.
5. Resolved. P7 keeps `products: ["CODEX"]`.
6. Resolved. P8 makes `au-*` optional with no WARN or FAIL when absent.
7. Resolved. P8 reports hook definitions and sends users to `/hooks`.
8. Resolved. P9 exercises marketplace add, plugin add, and installed-cache assertions.
9. Resolved. P10 adds reference, frontmatter, always-on, and upgrade coverage.
10. Not resolved. P10 does not require an old Codex trust record, prove one re-trust, or prove the Codex update preserves independently installed `au-*` plugins.

## Blocking

1. P3: “A user who compacts and quits ... resumes later with no copy.” This makes always-on silently disappear after a normal Codex `/compact`, exit, and resume. README recovery instructions do not restore the promised automatic behavior. Keep `resume` in the matcher until Codex exposes durable compact state, and add X7 to E2E.

2. P10: “checks ... hook definitions changed once.” A changed definition is not proof that an old trusted handler is rejected and one `/hooks` review restores it. The only retention test is `claude plugin prune`. Seed 0.2.2 Codex trust, assert upgraded hooks do not run until re-trusted, assert they run after one review and remain trusted on restart, and assert a manually installed `au-*` remains after the advertised Codex update commands.

## Optional

- Update the generated chunk footer. Its current “remove every opt-in flag” instruction conflicts with `ADHD_UNSLOP_ALWAYS=1` and `=off`. Add unit cases for both overrides.
- Test typed `au-unslop:unslop` too, not only `au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd`.
